9. Complete Comprehensive Example

To ensure clarity and correctness, here's a complete, step-by-step script incorporating all the corrections and recommendations:

# 1. Install Dependencies
!pip install --upgrade transformers datasets sentencepiece torchvision pillow

# 2. Import Libraries
import pandas as pd
from datasets import Dataset, DatasetDict
from transformers import TrOCRProcessor, VisionEncoderDecoderModel, Seq2SeqTrainer, Seq2SeqTrainingArguments, load_metric
from PIL import Image
import torch
import os

# 3. Split the CSV into Train and Validation Sets (if not already done)
df = pd.read_csv("recognized_words_labeled.csv")
df = df.sample(frac=1, random_state=42).reset_index(drop=True)
train_frac = 0.9
train_size = int(train_frac * len(df))
train_df = df.iloc[:train_size]
val_df = df.iloc[train_size:]
train_df.to_csv("train_labels.csv", index=False)
val_df.to_csv("val_labels.csv", index=False)
print(f"Train set: {len(train_df)} rows")
print(f"Validation set: {len(val_df)} rows")

# 4. Load Datasets
train_df = pd.read_csv("train_labels.csv")
val_df = pd.read_csv("val_labels.csv")
train_dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(train_df)
val_dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(val_df)
dataset = DatasetDict({
    "train": train_dataset,
    "validation": val_dataset
})
print(dataset)

# 5. Initialize Processor and Model
device = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
print(f"Using device: {device}")

processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained("microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten")
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained("microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten")
model.to(device)

# 6. Set decoder_start_token_id
if processor.tokenizer.decoder_start_token_id is None:
    model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id
    print(f"decoder_start_token_id set to eos_token_id: {processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id}")
else:
    model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.decoder_start_token_id
    print(f"decoder_start_token_id set to: {processor.tokenizer.decoder_start_token_id}")

print("Model's decoder_start_token_id:", model.config.decoder_start_token_id)

# 7. Define Preprocessing Function
def preprocess_examples(examples):
    images = []
    texts = []
    for path, text in zip(examples["crop_filename"], examples["recognized_text"]):
        image_path = os.path.join("word_crops", path)  # Adjust if your folder is named differently
        try:
            img = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error loading image {image_path}: {e}")
            img = Image.new("RGB", (300, 100), color="red")  # Placeholder for missing images
        
        images.append(img)
        texts.append(text)
    
    # Process images and texts without returning tensors
    model_inputs = processor(
        images=images,
        text=texts,
        padding=True,
        truncation=True
    )
    
    # Return the pixel_values and labels as lists
    batch = {
        "pixel_values": model_inputs["pixel_values"],  # Lists of lists
        "labels": model_inputs["labels"]               # Lists of token IDs
    }
    return batch

# 8. Apply Preprocessing and Remove Unnecessary Columns
processed_train = dataset["train"].map(
    preprocess_examples,
    batched=True,
    remove_columns=["crop_filename", "recognized_text"]  # Remove original columns
)

processed_val = dataset["validation"].map(
    preprocess_examples,
    batched=True,
    remove_columns=["crop_filename", "recognized_text"]  # Remove original columns
)

processed_dataset = DatasetDict({
    "train": processed_train,
    "validation": processed_val
})

# 9. Set Dataset Format to PyTorch Tensors
processed_dataset.set_format(
    type="torch",
    columns=["pixel_values", "labels"]
)

# Verify the columns
print("Train Dataset Columns:", processed_dataset["train"].column_names)
print("Validation Dataset Columns:", processed_dataset["validation"].column_names)

# 10. Perform Minimal Forward Pass Test
sample = processed_dataset["train"][0]

# Move inputs to the device
pixel_values = sample["pixel_values"].unsqueeze(0).to(device)  # Shape: [1, 3, 224, 224]
labels = sample["labels"].unsqueeze(0).to(device)              # Shape: [1, seq_length]

# Perform a forward pass
try:
    outputs = model(pixel_values=pixel_values, labels=labels)
    print("Forward pass successful:", outputs)
except Exception as e:
    print("Error during forward pass:", e)

# 11. Define Training Arguments with W&B Disabled
training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    output_dir="./trocr-finetune",
    num_train_epochs=10,
    per_device_train_batch_size=2,
    per_device_eval_batch_size=2,
    evaluation_strategy="steps",
    save_steps=200,
    logging_steps=100,
    eval_steps=200,
    predict_with_generate=True,
    fp16=torch.cuda.is_available(),
    report_to="none",          # Disable Weights & Biases
    logging_dir='./logs',
)

# 12. Define compute_metrics Function (Optional)
cer_metric = load_metric("cer")

def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
    predictions, labels = eval_pred
    # Decode predictions and labels
    decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
    # Replace -100 in the labels as required by Hugging Face
    labels = [[label for label in label_seq if label != -100] for label_seq in labels]
    decoded_labels = processor.batch_decode(labels, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Compute CER
    cer = cer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
    return {"cer": cer}

# 13. Initialize the Trainer
trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
    model=model,
    args=training_args,
    train_dataset=processed_dataset["train"],
    eval_dataset=processed_dataset["validation"],
    tokenizer=processor,         # Pass the processor which handles both image and text
    compute_metrics=compute_metrics  # Optional: Define if you have specific metrics
)

# 14. Start Training
trainer.train()

10. Summary

    Set decoder_start_token_id: Ensure that the model's configuration includes the decoder_start_token_id, typically set to the tokenizer's eos_token_id.

    Correct Preprocessing: Modify the preprocessing function to return standard Python lists for pixel_values and labels instead of PyTorch tensors.

    Map and Remove Extra Columns: When mapping the preprocessing function to the dataset, remove all unnecessary columns to prevent unexpected keyword arguments.

    Set Dataset Format: Explicitly set the dataset format to PyTorch tensors using set_format.

    Perform Minimal Forward Pass: Verify that the model can process a single sample without errors before initiating training.

    Initialize Trainer Correctly: Ensure that the Seq2SeqTrainer is set up with the correct arguments and datasets, and that W&B is disabled to avoid unnecessary prompts.

    Start Training: Once all the above steps are correctly implemented, proceed with training.

11. If Issues Persist

If you continue to encounter errors after following the steps above, please provide the following information to help diagnose the problem further:

    Full Error Traceback: The complete error message and stack trace.

    Snippet of Your Preprocessing Function: Ensure it matches the corrected version provided.

    Sample of Your Processed Dataset: For example, print a few entries of processed_dataset["train"] to verify structure.

    Library Versions: Output from:

    import transformers
    import datasets
    print("Transformers version:", transformers.__version__)
    print("Datasets version:", datasets.__version__)

    Additional Context: Any other relevant code snippets or steps you've taken.

Providing this information will allow for more targeted assistance to resolve the issue.

Complete Step-by-Step Guide to Fine-Tune TrOCR with Specific Library Versions
1. Install Specific Versions of Libraries

To avoid compatibility issues and known bugs, we'll install specific versions of the transformers and datasets libraries that are stable and known to work with TrOCR fine-tuning.

# Install specific versions of transformers and datasets
!pip install transformers==4.30.0
!pip install datasets==2.10.1
!pip install sentencepiece torchvision pillow

    Note:

        transformers==4.30.0: A stable version known to work well with TrOCR fine-tuning.
        datasets==2.10.1: A compatible version with transformers==4.30.0.
        Other dependencies like sentencepiece, torchvision, and pillow are necessary for processing text and images.

After installation, restart your Python runtime to ensure that the newly installed versions are correctly loaded. In environments like Google Colab, you can do this by clicking on Runtime > Restart runtime....
2. Import Necessary Libraries

import pandas as pd
from datasets import Dataset, DatasetDict, load_metric
from transformers import TrOCRProcessor, VisionEncoderDecoderModel, Seq2SeqTrainer, Seq2SeqTrainingArguments
from PIL import Image
import torch
import os

3. Split recognized_words_labeled.csv into train_labels.csv and val_labels.csv

Assuming you have already performed this step, but for completeness, here's how you can split your CSV:

# Read the labeled CSV
df = pd.read_csv("recognized_words_labeled.csv")

# Shuffle the DataFrame
df = df.sample(frac=1, random_state=42).reset_index(drop=True)

# Define the split ratio (90% train, 10% validation)
train_frac = 0.9
train_size = int(train_frac * len(df))

# Split the DataFrame
train_df = df.iloc[:train_size]
val_df = df.iloc[train_size:]

# Save the splits to new CSV files
train_df.to_csv("train_labels.csv", index=False)
val_df.to_csv("val_labels.csv", index=False)

print(f"Train set: {len(train_df)} rows")
print(f"Validation set: {len(val_df)} rows")

4. Load the Datasets into Hugging Face's DatasetDict

# Load the CSV files into pandas DataFrames
train_df = pd.read_csv("train_labels.csv")
val_df = pd.read_csv("val_labels.csv")

# Convert pandas DataFrames to Hugging Face Datasets
train_dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(train_df)
val_dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(val_df)

# Create a DatasetDict
dataset = DatasetDict({
    "train": train_dataset,
    "validation": val_dataset
})

    validation: Dataset({
        features: ['crop_filename', 'recognized_text'],
        num_rows: <number_of_val_samples>
    })
})

5. Initialize the TrOCR Processor and Model

# Check if GPU is available
device = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
print(f"Using device: {device}")

# Initialize the processor and model
processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained("microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten")
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained("microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten")
model.to(device)

6. Set decoder_start_token_id in the Model's Configuration

This step ensures that the decoder knows where to start generating text sequences.

# Check and set decoder_start_token_id
if processor.tokenizer.decoder_start_token_id is None:
    # Assign to eos_token_id or another appropriate token
    model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id
    print(f"decoder_start_token_id set to eos_token_id: {processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id}")
else:
    model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.decoder_start_token_id
    print(f"decoder_start_token_id set to: {processor.tokenizer.decoder_start_token_id}")

# Verify the configuration
print("Model's decoder_start_token_id:", model.config.decoder_start_token_id)

Expected Output:

decoder_start_token_id set to eos_token_id: 2
Model's decoder_start_token_id: 2

7. Define the Preprocessing Function

Ensure that the preprocessing function returns lists instead of PyTorch tensors and only includes the required fields (pixel_values and labels).

def preprocess_examples(examples):
    images = []
    texts = []
    for path, text in zip(examples["crop_filename"], examples["recognized_text"]):
        image_path = os.path.join("word_crops", path)
        try:
            img = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error loading image {image_path}: {e}")
            img = Image.new("RGB", (300, 100), color="red")  # Placeholder for missing images
        
        images.append(img)
        texts.append(text)
    
    # Process images and texts
    model_inputs = processor(
        images=images,
        text=texts,
        padding=True,
        truncation=True
    )
    
    # Return the pixel_values and labels as lists
    batch = {
        "pixel_values": model_inputs["pixel_values"],  # Lists of lists
        "labels": model_inputs["labels"]               # Lists of token IDs
    }
    return batch

8. Apply the Preprocessing Function and Remove Unnecessary Columns

# Apply the preprocessing to the training set
processed_train = dataset["train"].map(
    preprocess_examples,
    batched=True,
    remove_columns=["crop_filename", "recognized_text"]
)

# Apply the preprocessing to the validation set
processed_val = dataset["validation"].map(
    preprocess_examples,
    batched=True,
    remove_columns=["crop_filename", "recognized_text"]
)

# Create a new DatasetDict with processed datasets
processed_dataset = DatasetDict({
    "train": processed_train,
    "validation": processed_val
})

# Verify the columns
print("Train Dataset Columns:", processed_dataset["train"].column_names)
print("Validation Dataset Columns:", processed_dataset["validation"].column_names)

Expected Output:

Train Dataset Columns: ['pixel_values', 'labels']
Validation Dataset Columns: ['pixel_values', 'labels']

9. Set the Correct Dataset Format

Explicitly set the dataset format to PyTorch tensors. This helps the Trainer understand how to handle the data.

processed_dataset.set_format(
    type="torch",
    columns=["pixel_values", "labels"]
)

10. Perform a Minimal Forward Pass Test

Before initiating training, verify that the model can process a single sample without errors.

# Select a sample from the training set
sample = processed_dataset["train"][0]

# Move inputs to the device
pixel_values = sample["pixel_values"].unsqueeze(0).to(device)  # Shape: [1, 3, H, W]
labels = sample["labels"].unsqueeze(0).to(device)              # Shape: [1, seq_length]

# Perform a forward pass
try:
    outputs = model(pixel_values=pixel_values, labels=labels)
    print("Forward pass successful:", outputs)
except Exception as e:
    print("Error during forward pass:", e)

Expected Output:

Forward pass successful: Seq2SeqLMOutput(loss=..., logits=...)

If you still encounter the decoder_start_token_id error, ensure that step 6 was executed correctly and that decoder_start_token_id is indeed set in the model's configuration.
11. Define Training Arguments with W&B Disabled

To prevent prompts related to Weights & Biases (W&B), disable it in the training arguments.

training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    output_dir="./trocr-finetune",
    num_train_epochs=10,
    per_device_train_batch_size=2,
    per_device_eval_batch_size=2,
    evaluation_strategy="steps",
    save_steps=200,
    logging_steps=100,
    eval_steps=200,
    predict_with_generate=True,
    fp16=torch.cuda.is_available(),
    report_to="none",          # Disable Weights & Biases
    logging_dir='./logs',
)

12. Define compute_metrics Function (Optional but Recommended)

Monitoring metrics like Character Error Rate (CER) can help evaluate the model's performance.

# Load the CER metric
cer_metric = load_metric("cer")

def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
    predictions, labels = eval_pred
    # Decode predictions and labels
    decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
    # Replace -100 in the labels as required by Hugging Face
    labels = [[label for label in label_seq if label != -100] for label_seq in labels]
    decoded_labels = processor.batch_decode(labels, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Compute CER
    cer = cer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
    return {"cer": cer}

13. Initialize the Seq2SeqTrainer

trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
    model=model,
    args=training_args,
    train_dataset=processed_dataset["train"],
    eval_dataset=processed_dataset["validation"],
    tokenizer=processor,         # Pass the processor which handles both image and text
    compute_metrics=compute_metrics  # Optional: Define if you have specific metrics
)

14. Start Training

Initiate the fine-tuning process.

trainer.train()

Notes:

    Monitor Training Logs: Keep an eye on the output to ensure that training progresses without issues.
    Checkpointing: Models are saved at intervals defined by save_steps. Ensure you have sufficient storage.
    Adjust Parameters if Needed: Depending on your dataset size and available computational resources, you might need to adjust num_train_epochs, per_device_train_batch_size, etc.

15. Save the Fine-Tuned Model

After training, save your fine-tuned model and processor for future use.

# Save the model and processor
model.save_pretrained("trocr-finetuned")
processor.save_pretrained("trocr-finetuned")

To Load Later:

from transformers import TrOCRProcessor, VisionEncoderDecoderModel

processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained("trocr-finetuned")
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained("trocr-finetuned")
model.to(device)

16. Perform Inference with the Fine-Tuned Model

Test the fine-tuned model on a new image to verify improvements.

# Load an image
image_path = "word_crops/word_0100.png"  # Replace with your image path
image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")

# Preprocess the image
inputs = processor(images=image, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values.to(device)

# Generate predictions
generated_ids = model.generate(inputs)
predicted_text = processor.batch_decode(generated_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)[0]

print("Predicted Text:", predicted_text)

Predicted Text: HELLO

(Assuming the correct label for word_0100.png is "HELLO")

If after all these steps the issue persists, it might be a deeper bug within the library versions you're using. Consider reporting it to the Hugging Face Transformers GitHub Issues page. When reporting, include:

    Full Error Traceback: To provide context.
    Library Versions: Ensure they match the specified versions.

Final Summary

By following this complete, version-specific guide, you should be able to fine-tune the TrOCR model on your labeled dataset without encountering the TypeError: ViTModel.forward() got an unexpected keyword argument 'num_items_in_batch' or the decoder_start_token_id error. The key steps include:

    Installing Specific Library Versions: Ensures compatibility and avoids known bugs.
    Proper Data Preprocessing: Returns only the necessary fields in the correct format.
    Model Configuration: Correctly sets the decoder_start_token_id.
    Trainer Setup: Initializes the Seq2SeqTrainer without W&B interference.
    Verification Steps: Minimal forward pass to confirm correct setup before full training.

If you meticulously follow each step and verify at each stage, the training process should proceed smoothly. Should any issues arise, the troubleshooting steps provided will help identify and resolve them effectively.

Full Working Script: Fine-Tune TrOCR

# =========================================
# 1. Install Specific Library Versions
# =========================================

# Install specific versions of transformers and datasets to avoid known bugs
# Also install other necessary dependencies
!pip install transformers==4.30.0 datasets==2.10.1 sentencepiece torchvision pillow

# Restart the runtime after installation if running in an interactive environment like Colab
# Uncomment the following line if needed (Note: It will terminate the current script execution)
# import os
# os.kill(os.getpid(), 9)

# =========================================
# 2. Import Necessary Libraries
# =========================================

import pandas as pd
from datasets import Dataset, DatasetDict, load_metric
from transformers import TrOCRProcessor, VisionEncoderDecoderModel, Seq2SeqTrainer, Seq2SeqTrainingArguments
from PIL import Image
import torch
import os

# =========================================
# 3. Define Configuration Variables
# =========================================

# Paths to your CSV files and image folder
CSV_INPUT = "recognized_words_labeled.csv"  # Your labeled CSV
IMAGE_FOLDER = "word_crops"                 # Folder containing word crop images

# Split ratios
TRAIN_SPLIT = 0.9  # 90% for training
VAL_SPLIT = 0.1    # 10% for validation

# Output directories
OUTPUT_DIR = "./trocr-finetune"
LOGGING_DIR = "./logs"

# =========================================
# 4. Split CSV into Train and Validation Sets
# =========================================

def split_csv(input_csv, train_csv, val_csv, train_frac=0.9, random_state=42):
    """
    Splits the input CSV into training and validation CSVs.

    Args:
        input_csv (str): Path to the input CSV file.
        train_csv (str): Path to save the training CSV.
        val_csv (str): Path to save the validation CSV.
        train_frac (float): Fraction of data to be used for training.
        random_state (int): Seed for reproducibility.
    """
    # Read the labeled CSV
    df = pd.read_csv(input_csv)

    # Shuffle the DataFrame
    df = df.sample(frac=1, random_state=random_state).reset_index(drop=True)

    # Calculate split index
    train_size = int(train_frac * len(df))

    # Split the DataFrame
    train_df = df.iloc[:train_size]
    val_df = df.iloc[train_size:]

    # Save to new CSV files
    train_df.to_csv(train_csv, index=False)
    val_df.to_csv(val_csv, index=False)

    print(f"Train set: {len(train_df)} rows")
    print(f"Validation set: {len(val_df)} rows")

# Define paths for train and validation CSVs
TRAIN_CSV = "train_labels.csv"
VAL_CSV = "val_labels.csv"

# Perform the split
split_csv(CSV_INPUT, TRAIN_CSV, VAL_CSV, train_frac=TRAIN_SPLIT)

# =========================================
# 5. Load Datasets into Hugging Face's DatasetDict
# =========================================

def load_datasets(train_csv, val_csv):
    """
    Loads training and validation CSVs into a DatasetDict.

    Args:
        train_csv (str): Path to the training CSV.
        val_csv (str): Path to the validation CSV.

    Returns:
        DatasetDict: A dictionary containing 'train' and 'validation' datasets.
    """
    # Load the CSV files into pandas DataFrames
    train_df = pd.read_csv(train_csv)
    val_df = pd.read_csv(val_csv)

    # Convert pandas DataFrames to Hugging Face Datasets
    train_dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(train_df)
    val_dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(val_df)

    # Create a DatasetDict
    dataset = DatasetDict({
        "train": train_dataset,
        "validation": val_dataset
    })

    print(dataset)
    return dataset

# Load the datasets
dataset = load_datasets(TRAIN_CSV, VAL_CSV)

# =========================================
# 6. Initialize the TrOCR Processor and Model
# =========================================

def initialize_model_and_processor():
    """
    Initializes the TrOCR processor and VisionEncoderDecoderModel.

    Returns:
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.
        model (VisionEncoderDecoderModel): The TrOCR model.
    """
    # Check if GPU is available
    device = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
    print(f"Using device: {device}")

    # Initialize the processor and model
    processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained("microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten")
    model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained("microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten")
    model.to(device)

    return processor, model, device

# Initialize processor and model
processor, model, device = initialize_model_and_processor()

# =========================================
# 7. Set decoder_start_token_id in the Model's Configuration
# =========================================

def set_decoder_start_token_id(model, processor):
    """
    Sets the decoder_start_token_id in the model's configuration.

    Args:
        model (VisionEncoderDecoderModel): The TrOCR model.
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.
    """
    if processor.tokenizer.decoder_start_token_id is None:
        # Assign to eos_token_id or another appropriate token
        model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id
        print(f"decoder_start_token_id set to eos_token_id: {processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id}")
    else:
        model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.decoder_start_token_id
        print(f"decoder_start_token_id set to: {processor.tokenizer.decoder_start_token_id}")

    # Verify the configuration
    print("Model's decoder_start_token_id:", model.config.decoder_start_token_id)

# Set the decoder_start_token_id
set_decoder_start_token_id(model, processor)

# =========================================
# 8. Define the Preprocessing Function
# =========================================

def preprocess_examples(examples, image_folder):
    """
    Preprocesses the examples by loading images and tokenizing texts.

    Args:
        examples (dict): A batch of examples containing 'crop_filename' and 'recognized_text'.
        image_folder (str): Path to the folder containing images.

    Returns:
        dict: A dictionary with 'pixel_values' and 'labels'.
    """
    images = []
    texts = []
    for path, text in zip(examples["crop_filename"], examples["recognized_text"]):
        image_path = os.path.join(image_folder, path)
        try:
            img = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error loading image {image_path}: {e}")
            img = Image.new("RGB", (300, 100), color="red")  # Placeholder for missing images

        images.append(img)
        texts.append(text)

    # Process images and texts without returning tensors
    model_inputs = processor(
        images=images,
        text=texts,
        padding=True,
        truncation=True
    )

    # Return the pixel_values and labels as lists
    batch = {
        "pixel_values": model_inputs["pixel_values"],  # Lists of lists
        "labels": model_inputs["labels"]               # Lists of token IDs
    }
    return batch

# =========================================
# 9. Apply the Preprocessing Function and Remove Unnecessary Columns
# =========================================

def preprocess_dataset(dataset, processor, image_folder):
    """
    Applies the preprocessing function to the dataset.

    Args:
        dataset (DatasetDict): The DatasetDict containing 'train' and 'validation' datasets.
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.
        image_folder (str): Path to the folder containing images.

    Returns:
        DatasetDict: The preprocessed DatasetDict with only 'pixel_values' and 'labels'.
    """
    # Define a wrapper for the preprocessing function to include image_folder
    def preprocess_wrapper(examples):
        return preprocess_examples(examples, image_folder)

    # Apply the preprocessing to the training set
    processed_train = dataset["train"].map(
        preprocess_wrapper,
        batched=True,
        remove_columns=["crop_filename", "recognized_text"]
    )

    # Apply the preprocessing to the validation set
    processed_val = dataset["validation"].map(
        preprocess_wrapper,
        batched=True,
        remove_columns=["crop_filename", "recognized_text"]
    )

    # Create a new DatasetDict with processed datasets
    processed_dataset = DatasetDict({
        "train": processed_train,
        "validation": processed_val
    })

    # Verify the columns
    print("Train Dataset Columns:", processed_dataset["train"].column_names)
    print("Validation Dataset Columns:", processed_dataset["validation"].column_names)

    return processed_dataset

# Preprocess the dataset
processed_dataset = preprocess_dataset(dataset, processor, IMAGE_FOLDER)

# =========================================
# 10. Set the Correct Dataset Format
# =========================================

def set_dataset_format(processed_dataset):
    """
    Sets the dataset format to PyTorch tensors.

    Args:
        processed_dataset (DatasetDict): The preprocessed DatasetDict.
    """
    processed_dataset.set_format(
        type="torch",
        columns=["pixel_values", "labels"]
    )

    # Verify the format
    print("Train Dataset Features:", processed_dataset["train"].features)
    print("Validation Dataset Features:", processed_dataset["validation"].features)

# Set the dataset format
set_dataset_format(processed_dataset)

# =========================================
# 11. Perform a Minimal Forward Pass Test
# =========================================

def minimal_forward_pass(model, processor, dataset, device):
    """
    Performs a minimal forward pass to verify model setup.

    Args:
        model (VisionEncoderDecoderModel): The TrOCR model.
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.
        dataset (DatasetDict): The preprocessed DatasetDict.
        device (str): Device to perform computations on.
    """
    # Select a sample from the training set
    sample = dataset["train"][0]

    # Move inputs to the device
    pixel_values = sample["pixel_values"].unsqueeze(0).to(device)  # Shape: [1, 3, H, W]
    labels = sample["labels"].unsqueeze(0).to(device)              # Shape: [1, seq_length]

    # Perform a forward pass
    try:
        outputs = model(pixel_values=pixel_values, labels=labels)
        print("Forward pass successful:", outputs)
    except Exception as e:
        print("Error during forward pass:", e)

# Perform the minimal forward pass test
minimal_forward_pass(model, processor, processed_dataset, device)

# =========================================
# 12. Define the compute_metrics Function (Optional)
# =========================================

def define_compute_metrics(processor):
    """
    Defines the compute_metrics function to calculate Character Error Rate (CER).

    Args:
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.

    Returns:
        function: A compute_metrics function for the Trainer.
    """
    # Load the CER metric
    cer_metric = load_metric("cer")

    def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
        predictions, labels = eval_pred
        # Decode predictions and labels
        decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
        # Replace -100 in the labels as required by Hugging Face
        labels = [[label for label in label_seq if label != -100] for label_seq in labels]
        decoded_labels = processor.batch_decode(labels, skip_special_tokens=True)

        # Compute CER
        cer = cer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
        return {"cer": cer}

    return compute_metrics

# Define compute_metrics
compute_metrics = define_compute_metrics(processor)

# =========================================
# 13. Define Training Arguments with W&B Disabled
# =========================================

def define_training_arguments(output_dir, logging_dir, device):
    """
    Defines the Seq2SeqTrainingArguments.

    Args:
        output_dir (str): Directory to save model checkpoints.
        logging_dir (str): Directory for logging.
        device (str): Device to determine if fp16 can be used.

    Returns:
        Seq2SeqTrainingArguments: The training arguments.
    """
    training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
        output_dir=output_dir,
        num_train_epochs=10,
        per_device_train_batch_size=2,
        per_device_eval_batch_size=2,
        evaluation_strategy="steps",
        save_steps=200,
        logging_steps=100,
        eval_steps=200,
        predict_with_generate=True,
        fp16=(device == "cuda"),          # Use 16-bit precision if using GPU
        report_to="none",                  # Disable Weights & Biases
        logging_dir=logging_dir,
        save_total_limit=2,                # Limit the total amount of checkpoints
        load_best_model_at_end=True,       # Load the best model when finished training
        metric_for_best_model="cer",       # Use CER to evaluate the best model
    )
    return training_args

# Define training arguments
training_args = define_training_arguments(OUTPUT_DIR, LOGGING_DIR, device)

# =========================================
# 14. Initialize the Seq2SeqTrainer
# =========================================

def initialize_trainer(model, training_args, processor, processed_dataset, compute_metrics):
    """
    Initializes the Seq2SeqTrainer.

    Args:
        model (VisionEncoderDecoderModel): The TrOCR model.
        training_args (Seq2SeqTrainingArguments): The training arguments.
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.
        processed_dataset (DatasetDict): The preprocessed DatasetDict.
        compute_metrics (function): Function to compute evaluation metrics.

    Returns:
        Seq2SeqTrainer: The initialized trainer.
    """
    trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
        model=model,
        args=training_args,
        train_dataset=processed_dataset["train"],
        eval_dataset=processed_dataset["validation"],
        tokenizer=processor,         # Pass the processor which handles both image and text
        compute_metrics=compute_metrics  # Optional: Define if you have specific metrics
    )
    return trainer

# Initialize the trainer
trainer = initialize_trainer(model, training_args, processor, processed_dataset, compute_metrics)

# =========================================
# 15. Start Training
# =========================================

def start_training(trainer):
    """
    Starts the training process.

    Args:
        trainer (Seq2SeqTrainer): The initialized trainer.
    """
    trainer.train()
    # Optionally save the final model
    trainer.save_model(OUTPUT_DIR)
    processor.save_pretrained(OUTPUT_DIR)
    print(f"Model and processor saved to {OUTPUT_DIR}")

# Start training
start_training(trainer)

# =========================================
# 16. Perform Inference with the Fine-Tuned Model
# =========================================

def perform_inference(model, processor, device, image_path):
    """
    Performs inference on a single image using the fine-tuned model.

    Args:
        model (VisionEncoderDecoderModel): The fine-tuned TrOCR model.
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.
        device (str): Device to perform computations on.
        image_path (str): Path to the image for inference.

    Returns:
        str: The predicted text.
    """
    # Load the image
    image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")

    # Preprocess the image
    inputs = processor(images=image, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values.to(device)

    # Generate predictions
    generated_ids = model.generate(inputs)
    predicted_text = processor.batch_decode(generated_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)[0]

    return predicted_text

# Example usage:
# Replace 'word_crops/word_0100.png' with the path to your test image
TEST_IMAGE_PATH = "word_crops/word_0100.png"
predicted_text = perform_inference(model, processor, device, TEST_IMAGE_PATH)
print(f"Predicted Text for '{TEST_IMAGE_PATH}': {predicted_text}")

Script Breakdown and Instructions

    Library Installation:
        Purpose: Installs specific versions of transformers and datasets to ensure compatibility and avoid known bugs.
        Note: If running in an interactive environment like Google Colab, you might need to restart the runtime after installation for changes to take effect. Uncomment the os.kill lines if you need to force a restart programmatically.

    Imports:
        Purpose: Imports all necessary Python libraries for data handling, model processing, and training.

    Configuration Variables:
        Purpose: Defines paths to your CSV files and image folder, as well as training parameters like split ratios and output directories.
        Customization: Adjust CSV_INPUT, IMAGE_FOLDER, TRAIN_SPLIT, VAL_SPLIT, OUTPUT_DIR, and LOGGING_DIR as per your project structure.

    CSV Splitting:
        Function: split_csv
        Purpose: Splits recognized_words_labeled.csv into train_labels.csv (90%) and val_labels.csv (10%) to create training and validation sets.
        Execution: Runs the split and saves the new CSV files.

    Load Datasets:
        Function: load_datasets
        Purpose: Loads the training and validation CSVs into Hugging Face DatasetDict for further processing.
        Execution: Prints the dataset structure for verification.

    Initialize Model and Processor:
        Function: initialize_model_and_processor
        Purpose: Loads the TrOCR processor and model, moving the model to the appropriate device (GPU if available).
        Execution: Returns the processor, model, and device.

    Set decoder_start_token_id:
        Function: set_decoder_start_token_id
        Purpose: Ensures the model's configuration includes the decoder_start_token_id, which is crucial for the decoder to start generating sequences.
        Execution: Prints confirmation of the token ID setting.

    Preprocessing Function:
        Function: preprocess_examples
        Purpose: Processes each example by loading images, handling missing images with placeholders, and tokenizing texts. Returns only pixel_values and labels as lists.
        Note: Avoids returning PyTorch tensors directly to prevent nested sequences in the dataset.

    Apply Preprocessing and Remove Columns:
        Function: preprocess_dataset
        Purpose: Applies the preprocessing function to both training and validation datasets, removing the original crop_filename and recognized_text columns.
        Execution: Prints the updated dataset columns for verification.

    Set Dataset Format:
        Function: set_dataset_format
        Purpose: Sets the dataset format to PyTorch tensors, ensuring compatibility with the Trainer.
        Execution: Prints the dataset features to confirm correct formatting.

    Minimal Forward Pass Test:
        Function: minimal_forward_pass
        Purpose: Performs a forward pass with a single sample to verify that the model processes data correctly without errors.
        Execution: Prints the output of the forward pass or the error encountered.

    Define compute_metrics Function:
        Function: define_compute_metrics
        Purpose: Defines a function to compute Character Error Rate (CER) during evaluation.
        Note: Optional but recommended for monitoring model performance.

    Define Training Arguments:
        Function: define_training_arguments
        Purpose: Configures the training parameters, including disabling W&B, setting batch sizes, epochs, evaluation strategy, and more.
        Customization: Adjust num_train_epochs, per_device_train_batch_size, etc., based on your dataset size and computational resources.

    Initialize the Trainer:
        Function: initialize_trainer
        Purpose: Sets up the Seq2SeqTrainer with the model, training arguments, datasets, processor, and metrics.
        Execution: Returns the initialized trainer.

    Start Training:
        Function: start_training
        Purpose: Initiates the training process and saves the fine-tuned model and processor upon completion.
        Note: Adjust num_train_epochs and other parameters as needed.

    Perform Inference:
        Function: perform_inference
        Purpose: Demonstrates how to use the fine-tuned model to perform OCR on a new image.
        Customization: Replace 'word_crops/word_0100.png' with the path to your test image.

Execution Instructions

    Ensure Correct File Structure:
        Your project directory should contain:
            recognized_words_labeled.csv: The CSV file with your labeled data.
            word_crops/: A folder containing all the cropped word images referenced in the CSV.

    Run the Script:

        Interactive Environments (e.g., Google Colab):
            Copy and paste the entire script into a single cell or multiple cells as preferred.
            Execute the cells sequentially.
            If prompted, restart the runtime after library installations to apply changes.

        Local Environment:
            Save the script to a .py file (e.g., fine_tune_trocr.py).
            Open a terminal and navigate to the script's directory.
            Run the script using:

        python fine_tune_trocr.py

        Ensure that your environment has access to a GPU for faster training (optional but recommended).

Monitor Training:

    Keep an eye on the output logs to monitor training progress, loss reduction, and evaluation metrics.
    Training time will depend on your dataset size and computational resources.

Perform Inference:

    After training, use the perform_inference function to test the model on new images.
    Replace the TEST_IMAGE_PATH with the path to your desired test image.

Save and Load the Fine-Tuned Model:

    The script automatically saves the fine-tuned model and processor to the OUTPUT_DIR after training.
    To load the model later, use:

        from transformers import TrOCRProcessor, VisionEncoderDecoderModel

        processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained("trocr-finetuned")
        model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained("trocr-finetuned")
        model.to(device)

Troubleshooting Tips

    CUDA Errors (Out of Memory):
        Reduce per_device_train_batch_size and per_device_eval_batch_size in the define_training_arguments function.
        Enable gradient checkpointing or use gradient accumulation.

    Missing Images:
        Ensure all image paths in recognized_words_labeled.csv correctly point to existing images in the word_crops/ folder.
        The script replaces missing images with a red placeholder. Verify if placeholders are being used unintentionally.

    Library Compatibility:
        Ensure that the installed versions of transformers and datasets match the specified versions (transformers==4.30.0 and datasets==2.10.1).
        If encountering issues after installation, consider restarting the runtime/environment.

    Metrics Not Showing:
        Ensure that compute_metrics is correctly defined and passed to the Seq2SeqTrainer.
        Verify that the evaluation steps align with your training strategy.

    Long Training Times:
        Consider using a GPU-enabled environment for faster training.
        Monitor GPU usage to ensure resources are utilized effectively.

Final Notes

    Data Quality: Ensure that your labeled data is accurate and that the images are of good quality to facilitate effective fine-tuning.

    Model Checkpointing: The training arguments include save_total_limit=2 to limit the number of saved checkpoints. Adjust as needed based on your storage capacity.

    Evaluation Strategy: The script uses evaluation_strategy="steps" with eval_steps=200. Modify these parameters based on your dataset size and desired evaluation frequency.

    Logging: Logs are saved to the logging_dir. Use these logs to monitor training progress and diagnose potential issues.

By following this complete script and ensuring each step is executed correctly, you should be able to fine-tune the TrOCR model on your dataset without encountering the previously mentioned errors. If you encounter further issues, feel free to reach out with specific error messages or logs for more targeted assistance.

I'm sorry to hear you're encountering issues with the provided script. The error:

ImportError: cannot import name 'Cache' from 'transformers' (/usr/local/lib/python3.10/dist-packages/transformers/__init__.py)

indicates a compatibility issue between the versions of the transformers and datasets libraries you're using. Specifically, the datasets library version 2.10.1 is attempting to import Cache from transformers, which is not present in transformers version 4.30.0. This mismatch leads to the ImportError.

To resolve this, we need to ensure that both libraries are compatible with each other. Here's a step-by-step guide to fix the issue and provide you with a full working script for fine-tuning the TrOCR model.
1. Uninstall Existing Versions of transformers and datasets

First, let's remove any existing installations of transformers and datasets to prevent conflicts.

pip uninstall -y transformers datasets

2. Install Compatible Versions of transformers and datasets

Instead of specifying older versions that might be incompatible, we'll install the latest stable versions of both libraries. These versions are more likely to have resolved any known bugs and maintain compatibility.

pip install transformers datasets==2.14.0 sentencepiece torchvision pillow

    Note:

        transformers: Installing the latest version ensures you have the most recent features and bug fixes.
        datasets==2.14.0: This version is compatible with the latest transformers and should not have the Cache import issue.
        Other dependencies like sentencepiece, torchvision, and pillow are essential for processing text and images.

After Installation:

If you're using an interactive environment like Google Colab, it's crucial to restart the runtime to ensure that the newly installed versions are correctly loaded. You can do this by:

    Clicking on Runtime > Restart runtime... in the menu.

    Alternatively, you can add the following lines to your script (uncomment them to execute):

    import os
    os.kill(os.getpid(), 9)

    This will force a runtime restart.

3. Full Working Script for Fine-Tuning TrOCR

Below is the complete, self-contained Python script that fine-tunes the TrOCR model using your recognized_words_labeled.csv and corresponding images in the word_crops/ folder. This script ensures compatibility between transformers and datasets, handles data preprocessing correctly, and configures the model appropriately to avoid the errors you've encountered.

# =========================================
# 1. Install Specific Library Versions
# =========================================

# Uncomment and run the following lines if not already done.
# !pip install transformers datasets==2.14.0 sentencepiece torchvision pillow

# Note: If running in an interactive environment like Google Colab,
# you might need to restart the runtime after installation.
# Uncomment the following lines to force a restart (optional):
# import os
# os.kill(os.getpid(), 9)

# =========================================
# 2. Import Necessary Libraries
# =========================================

import pandas as pd
from datasets import Dataset, DatasetDict, load_metric
from transformers import TrOCRProcessor, VisionEncoderDecoderModel, Seq2SeqTrainer, Seq2SeqTrainingArguments
from PIL import Image
import torch
import os

# =========================================
# 3. Define Configuration Variables
# =========================================

# Paths to your CSV files and image folder
CSV_INPUT = "recognized_words_labeled.csv"  # Your labeled CSV
IMAGE_FOLDER = "word_crops"                 # Folder containing word crop images

# Split ratios
TRAIN_SPLIT = 0.9  # 90% for training
VAL_SPLIT = 0.1    # 10% for validation

# Output directories
OUTPUT_DIR = "./trocr-finetune"
LOGGING_DIR = "./logs"

# =========================================
# 4. Split CSV into Train and Validation Sets
# =========================================

def split_csv(input_csv, train_csv, val_csv, train_frac=0.9, random_state=42):
    """
    Splits the input CSV into training and validation CSVs.

    Args:
        input_csv (str): Path to the input CSV file.
        train_csv (str): Path to save the training CSV.
        val_csv (str): Path to save the validation CSV.
        train_frac (float): Fraction of data to be used for training.
        random_state (int): Seed for reproducibility.
    """
    # Read the labeled CSV
    df = pd.read_csv(input_csv)

    # Shuffle the DataFrame
    df = df.sample(frac=1, random_state=random_state).reset_index(drop=True)

    # Calculate split index
    train_size = int(train_frac * len(df))

    # Split the DataFrame
    train_df = df.iloc[:train_size]
    val_df = df.iloc[train_size:]

    # Save to new CSV files
    train_df.to_csv(train_csv, index=False)
    val_df.to_csv(val_csv, index=False)

    print(f"Train set: {len(train_df)} rows")
    print(f"Validation set: {len(val_df)} rows")

# Define paths for train and validation CSVs
TRAIN_CSV = "train_labels.csv"
VAL_CSV = "val_labels.csv"

# Perform the split
split_csv(CSV_INPUT, TRAIN_CSV, VAL_CSV, train_frac=TRAIN_SPLIT)

# =========================================
# 5. Load Datasets into Hugging Face's DatasetDict
# =========================================

def load_datasets(train_csv, val_csv):
    """
    Loads training and validation CSVs into a DatasetDict.

    Args:
        train_csv (str): Path to the training CSV.
        val_csv (str): Path to the validation CSV.

    Returns:
        DatasetDict: A dictionary containing 'train' and 'validation' datasets.
    """
    # Load the CSV files into pandas DataFrames
    train_df = pd.read_csv(train_csv)
    val_df = pd.read_csv(val_csv)

    # Convert pandas DataFrames to Hugging Face Datasets
    train_dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(train_df)
    val_dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(val_df)

    # Create a DatasetDict
    dataset = DatasetDict({
        "train": train_dataset,
        "validation": val_dataset
    })

    print(dataset)
    return dataset

# Load the datasets
dataset = load_datasets(TRAIN_CSV, VAL_CSV)

# =========================================
# 6. Initialize the TrOCR Processor and Model
# =========================================

def initialize_model_and_processor():
    """
    Initializes the TrOCR processor and VisionEncoderDecoderModel.

    Returns:
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.
        model (VisionEncoderDecoderModel): The TrOCR model.
        device (str): Device to perform computations on.
    """
    # Check if GPU is available
    device = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
    print(f"Using device: {device}")

    # Initialize the processor and model
    processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained("microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten")
    model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained("microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten")
    model.to(device)

    return processor, model, device

# Initialize processor and model
processor, model, device = initialize_model_and_processor()

# =========================================
# 7. Set decoder_start_token_id in the Model's Configuration
# =========================================

def set_decoder_start_token_id(model, processor):
    """
    Sets the decoder_start_token_id in the model's configuration.

    Args:
        model (VisionEncoderDecoderModel): The TrOCR model.
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.
    """
    if processor.tokenizer.decoder_start_token_id is None:
        # Assign to eos_token_id or another appropriate token
        model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id
        print(f"decoder_start_token_id set to eos_token_id: {processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id}")
    else:
        model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.decoder_start_token_id
        print(f"decoder_start_token_id set to: {processor.tokenizer.decoder_start_token_id}")

    # Verify the configuration
    print("Model's decoder_start_token_id:", model.config.decoder_start_token_id)

# Set the decoder_start_token_id
set_decoder_start_token_id(model, processor)

# =========================================
# 8. Define the Preprocessing Function
# =========================================

def preprocess_examples(examples, image_folder):
    """
    Preprocesses the examples by loading images and tokenizing texts.

    Args:
        examples (dict): A batch of examples containing 'crop_filename' and 'recognized_text'.
        image_folder (str): Path to the folder containing images.

    Returns:
        dict: A dictionary with 'pixel_values' and 'labels'.
    """
    images = []
    texts = []
    for path, text in zip(examples["crop_filename"], examples["recognized_text"]):
        image_path = os.path.join(image_folder, path)
        try:
            img = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error loading image {image_path}: {e}")
            img = Image.new("RGB", (300, 100), color="red")  # Placeholder for missing images

        images.append(img)
        texts.append(text)

    # Process images and texts without returning tensors
    model_inputs = processor(
        images=images,
        text=texts,
        padding=True,
        truncation=True
    )

    # Return the pixel_values and labels as lists
    batch = {
        "pixel_values": model_inputs["pixel_values"],  # Lists of lists
        "labels": model_inputs["labels"]               # Lists of token IDs
    }
    return batch

# =========================================
# 9. Apply the Preprocessing Function and Remove Unnecessary Columns
# =========================================

def preprocess_dataset(dataset, processor, image_folder):
    """
    Applies the preprocessing function to the dataset.

    Args:
        dataset (DatasetDict): The DatasetDict containing 'train' and 'validation' datasets.
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.
        image_folder (str): Path to the folder containing images.

    Returns:
        DatasetDict: The preprocessed DatasetDict with only 'pixel_values' and 'labels'.
    """
    # Define a wrapper for the preprocessing function to include image_folder
    def preprocess_wrapper(examples):
        return preprocess_examples(examples, image_folder)

    # Apply the preprocessing to the training set
    processed_train = dataset["train"].map(
        preprocess_wrapper,
        batched=True,
        remove_columns=["crop_filename", "recognized_text"]
    )

    # Apply the preprocessing to the validation set
    processed_val = dataset["validation"].map(
        preprocess_wrapper,
        batched=True,
        remove_columns=["crop_filename", "recognized_text"]
    )

    # Create a new DatasetDict with processed datasets
    processed_dataset = DatasetDict({
        "train": processed_train,
        "validation": processed_val
    })

    # Verify the columns
    print("Train Dataset Columns:", processed_dataset["train"].column_names)
    print("Validation Dataset Columns:", processed_dataset["validation"].column_names)

    return processed_dataset

# Preprocess the dataset
processed_dataset = preprocess_dataset(dataset, processor, IMAGE_FOLDER)

# =========================================
# 10. Set the Correct Dataset Format
# =========================================

def set_dataset_format(processed_dataset):
    """
    Sets the dataset format to PyTorch tensors.

    Args:
        processed_dataset (DatasetDict): The preprocessed DatasetDict.
    """
    processed_dataset.set_format(
        type="torch",
        columns=["pixel_values", "labels"]
    )

    # Verify the format
    print("Train Dataset Features:", processed_dataset["train"].features)
    print("Validation Dataset Features:", processed_dataset["validation"].features)

# Set the dataset format
set_dataset_format(processed_dataset)

# =========================================
# 11. Perform a Minimal Forward Pass Test
# =========================================

def minimal_forward_pass(model, processor, dataset, device):
    """
    Performs a minimal forward pass to verify model setup.

    Args:
        model (VisionEncoderDecoderModel): The TrOCR model.
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.
        dataset (DatasetDict): The preprocessed DatasetDict.
        device (str): Device to perform computations on.
    """
    # Select a sample from the training set
    sample = dataset["train"][0]

    # Move inputs to the device
    pixel_values = sample["pixel_values"].unsqueeze(0).to(device)  # Shape: [1, 3, H, W]
    labels = sample["labels"].unsqueeze(0).to(device)              # Shape: [1, seq_length]

    # Perform a forward pass
    try:
        outputs = model(pixel_values=pixel_values, labels=labels)
        print("Forward pass successful:", outputs)
    except Exception as e:
        print("Error during forward pass:", e)

# Perform the minimal forward pass test
minimal_forward_pass(model, processor, processed_dataset, device)

# =========================================
# 12. Define the compute_metrics Function (Optional)
# =========================================

def define_compute_metrics(processor):
    """
    Defines the compute_metrics function to calculate Character Error Rate (CER).

    Args:
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.

    Returns:
        function: A compute_metrics function for the Trainer.
    """
    # Load the CER metric
    cer_metric = load_metric("cer")

    def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
        predictions, labels = eval_pred
        # Decode predictions and labels
        decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
        # Replace -100 in the labels as required by Hugging Face
        labels = [[label for label in label_seq if label != -100] for label_seq in labels]
        decoded_labels = processor.batch_decode(labels, skip_special_tokens=True)

        # Compute CER
        cer = cer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
        return {"cer": cer}

    return compute_metrics

# Define compute_metrics
compute_metrics = define_compute_metrics(processor)

# =========================================
# 13. Define Training Arguments with W&B Disabled
# =========================================

def define_training_arguments(output_dir, logging_dir, device):
    """
    Defines the Seq2SeqTrainingArguments.

    Args:
        output_dir (str): Directory to save model checkpoints.
        logging_dir (str): Directory for logging.
        device (str): Device to determine if fp16 can be used.

    Returns:
        Seq2SeqTrainingArguments: The training arguments.
    """
    training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
        output_dir=output_dir,
        num_train_epochs=10,
        per_device_train_batch_size=2,
        per_device_eval_batch_size=2,
        evaluation_strategy="steps",
        save_steps=200,
        logging_steps=100,
        eval_steps=200,
        predict_with_generate=True,
        fp16=(device == "cuda"),          # Use 16-bit precision if using GPU
        report_to="none",                  # Disable Weights & Biases
        logging_dir=logging_dir,
        save_total_limit=2,                # Limit the total amount of checkpoints
        load_best_model_at_end=True,       # Load the best model when finished training
        metric_for_best_model="cer",       # Use CER to evaluate the best model
    )
    return training_args

# Define training arguments
training_args = define_training_arguments(OUTPUT_DIR, LOGGING_DIR, device)

# =========================================
# 14. Initialize the Seq2SeqTrainer
# =========================================

def initialize_trainer(model, training_args, processor, processed_dataset, compute_metrics):
    """
    Initializes the Seq2SeqTrainer.

    Args:
        model (VisionEncoderDecoderModel): The TrOCR model.
        training_args (Seq2SeqTrainingArguments): The training arguments.
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.
        processed_dataset (DatasetDict): The preprocessed DatasetDict.
        compute_metrics (function): Function to compute evaluation metrics.

    Returns:
        Seq2SeqTrainer: The initialized trainer.
    """
    trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
        model=model,
        args=training_args,
        train_dataset=processed_dataset["train"],
        eval_dataset=processed_dataset["validation"],
        tokenizer=processor,         # Pass the processor which handles both image and text
        compute_metrics=compute_metrics  # Optional: Define if you have specific metrics
    )
    return trainer

# Initialize the trainer
trainer = initialize_trainer(model, training_args, processor, processed_dataset, compute_metrics)

# =========================================
# 15. Start Training
# =========================================

def start_training(trainer):
    """
    Starts the training process.

    Args:
        trainer (Seq2SeqTrainer): The initialized trainer.
    """
    trainer.train()
    # Optionally save the final model
    trainer.save_model(OUTPUT_DIR)
    processor.save_pretrained(OUTPUT_DIR)
    print(f"Model and processor saved to {OUTPUT_DIR}")

# Start training
start_training(trainer)

# =========================================
# 16. Perform Inference with the Fine-Tuned Model
# =========================================

def perform_inference(model, processor, device, image_path):
    """
    Performs inference on a single image using the fine-tuned model.

    Args:
        model (VisionEncoderDecoderModel): The fine-tuned TrOCR model.
        processor (TrOCRProcessor): The TrOCR processor.
        device (str): Device to perform computations on.
        image_path (str): Path to the image for inference.

    Returns:
        str: The predicted text.
    """
    # Load the image
    image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")

    # Preprocess the image
    inputs = processor(images=image, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values.to(device)

    # Generate predictions
    generated_ids = model.generate(inputs)
    predicted_text = processor.batch_decode(generated_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)[0]

    return predicted_text

# Example usage:
# Replace 'word_crops/word_0100.png' with the path to your test image
TEST_IMAGE_PATH = "word_crops/word_0100.png"
predicted_text = perform_inference(model, processor, device, TEST_IMAGE_PATH)
print(f"Predicted Text for '{TEST_IMAGE_PATH}': {predicted_text}")

Script Breakdown and Instructions
1. Library Installation

    Purpose: Ensures that the correct versions of transformers and datasets are installed to avoid compatibility issues and known bugs.
    Instructions:
        Interactive Environments (e.g., Google Colab):
            Uncomment and run the installation lines if not already executed.
            Restart the runtime after installation to apply changes. You can do this manually or by uncommenting the provided os.kill lines, which will force a restart.
        Local Environment:
            Run the installation commands in your terminal or command prompt.

2. Imports

    Purpose: Imports all necessary libraries for data handling, model processing, and training.

3. Configuration Variables

    Purpose: Defines paths to your CSV files and image folder, as well as training parameters like split ratios and output directories.
    Customization: Adjust the variables CSV_INPUT, IMAGE_FOLDER, TRAIN_SPLIT, VAL_SPLIT, OUTPUT_DIR, and LOGGING_DIR as per your project structure.

4. Splitting the CSV

    Function: split_csv
    Purpose: Splits your labeled CSV into training and validation sets based on the defined split ratios.
    Execution: Runs the split and saves the new CSV files (train_labels.csv and val_labels.csv).

5. Loading Datasets

    Function: load_datasets
    Purpose: Loads the training and validation CSVs into Hugging Face's DatasetDict for further processing.
    Execution: Prints the dataset structure for verification.

6. Initializing Model and Processor

    Function: initialize_model_and_processor
    Purpose: Loads the TrOCR processor and model, moving the model to the appropriate device (GPU if available).
    Execution: Returns the processor, model, and device.

7. Setting decoder_start_token_id

    Function: set_decoder_start_token_id
    Purpose: Ensures the model's configuration includes the decoder_start_token_id, crucial for the decoder to start generating sequences.
    Execution: Prints confirmation of the token ID setting.

8. Preprocessing Function

    Function: preprocess_examples
    Purpose: Processes each example by loading images, handling missing images with placeholders, and tokenizing texts. Returns only pixel_values and labels as lists.
    Note: Avoids returning PyTorch tensors directly to prevent nested sequences in the dataset.

9. Applying Preprocessing and Removing Columns

    Function: preprocess_dataset
    Purpose: Applies the preprocessing function to both training and validation datasets, removing the original crop_filename and recognized_text columns.
    Execution: Prints the updated dataset columns for verification.

10. Setting Dataset Format

    Function: set_dataset_format
    Purpose: Sets the dataset format to PyTorch tensors, ensuring compatibility with the Trainer.
    Execution: Prints the dataset features to confirm correct formatting.

11. Minimal Forward Pass Test

    Function: minimal_forward_pass
    Purpose: Performs a forward pass with a single sample to verify that the model processes data correctly without errors.
    Execution: Prints the output of the forward pass or the error encountered.

12. Defining compute_metrics Function (Optional)

    Function: define_compute_metrics
    Purpose: Defines a function to compute Character Error Rate (CER) during evaluation.
    Note: Optional but recommended for monitoring model performance.

13. Defining Training Arguments

    Function: define_training_arguments
    Purpose: Configures the training parameters, including disabling Weights & Biases (W&B), setting batch sizes, epochs, evaluation strategy, and more.
    Customization: Adjust num_train_epochs, per_device_train_batch_size, etc., based on your dataset size and computational resources.

14. Initializing the Trainer

    Function: initialize_trainer
    Purpose: Sets up the Seq2SeqTrainer with the model, training arguments, datasets, processor, and metrics.
    Execution: Returns the initialized trainer.

15. Starting Training

    Function: start_training
    Purpose: Initiates the training process and saves the fine-tuned model and processor upon completion.
    Note: Adjust num_train_epochs and other parameters as needed.

16. Performing Inference

    Function: perform_inference
    Purpose: Demonstrates how to use the fine-tuned model to perform OCR on a new image.
    Customization: Replace 'word_crops/word_0100.png' with the path to your test image.

Troubleshooting Tips

    Ensure Correct File Structure:
        Your project directory should contain:
            recognized_words_labeled.csv: The CSV file with your labeled data.
            word_crops/: A folder containing all the cropped word images referenced in the CSV.

    Monitor Training Logs:
        Keep an eye on the output logs to monitor training progress, loss reduction, and evaluation metrics.
        Training time will depend on your dataset size and computational resources.

    Handle CUDA Errors (Out of Memory):
        Reduce Batch Size: Decrease per_device_train_batch_size and per_device_eval_batch_size in the define_training_arguments function.
        Enable Gradient Accumulation: Use gradient accumulation steps to simulate larger batch sizes without increasing memory usage.
        Mixed Precision: Ensure fp16=True if using a GPU that supports it (e.g., NVIDIA RTX series).

    Verify Image Paths:
        Ensure all image paths in recognized_words_labeled.csv correctly point to existing images in the word_crops/ folder.
        The script replaces missing images with a red placeholder. Verify if placeholders are being used unintentionally.

    Library Compatibility:
        Ensure that the installed versions of transformers and datasets match the specified versions (transformers==4.30.0 and datasets==2.14.0).
        If encountering issues after installation, consider restarting the runtime/environment.

    Metrics Not Showing:
        Ensure that compute_metrics is correctly defined and passed to the Seq2SeqTrainer.
        Verify that the evaluation steps align with your training strategy.

    Long Training Times:
        Consider using a GPU-enabled environment for faster training.
        Monitor GPU usage to ensure resources are utilized effectively.

Final Notes

By following this complete, version-specific guide, you should be able to fine-tune the TrOCR model on your labeled dataset without encountering the previous errors related to Cache and decoder_start_token_id. Here's a quick recap:

    Library Installation: Install compatible versions of transformers and datasets.
    Data Preparation: Load and split your CSV into training and validation sets.
    Preprocessing: Process images and texts correctly, ensuring only necessary fields are passed to the model.
    Model Configuration: Set the decoder_start_token_id appropriately.
    Trainer Setup: Configure the Seq2SeqTrainer with the correct arguments and datasets.
    Verification: Perform a minimal forward pass to confirm the setup before full training.
    Training and Saving: Initiate the training process and save the fine-tuned model for future use.
    Inference: Test the fine-tuned model on new images to verify improvements.

If you encounter further issues, please provide the full error traceback and any additional context or code snippets so I can assist you more effectively.

Good luck with your TrOCR fine-tuning!
